## 1. Optimal Computation From Fluctuation Responses

- **Tags**: `thermodynamics`  `stochastic thermodynamics`  `Landauer's principle`  `fluctuation theorems`  `energy cost of computation`

### TL;DR

Derives fundamental thermodynamic bounds on the energy cost of optimal computation using fluctuation-response relations from stochastic thermodynamics.

### Motivation

The energy cost of computation is a central challenge; previous bounds like Landauer's limit are insufficient for realistic finite-time, fluctuating processes.

### Method

Applies fluctuation-response theory and stochastic thermodynamics to model computation as a physical process, deriving trade-offs between work, dissipation, and computation rate.

### Result

Establishes optimal computation bounds that generalize Landauer's principle to finite-time regimes, showing how fluctuations constrain minimal energy dissipation.

### Conclusion

Optimal computation requires balancing speed, accuracy, and energy cost, with fundamental limits set by thermodynamic fluctuation-response relations.

*Keywords: stochastic thermodynamics, Landauer's principle, fluctuation theorems, energy cost of computation, thermodynamic bounds*

### TL;DR

Demonstrates high-fidelity entanglement and coherent multi-qubit mapping between different qubit types in an atom array.

### Motivation

Different qubit encodings (e.g., hyperfine, Rydberg) offer complementary advantages; coherently transferring entangled states between them enables more flexible quantum information processing.

### Result

### Conclusion

Establishes a versatile platform for hybrid quantum information processing, combining the strengths of different qubit encodings.

## 3. Coupling A $^{73}\mathrm{Ge}$ Nuclear Spin To An Electrostatically Defined Quantum Dot In Silicon

Hudson, Kok Wai Chan, Christopher C. Escott, Andre Saraiva, Chih Hwan Yang, Vincent Mourik, Andrea Morello, Andrew S. Dzurak, And Arne Laucht
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/49gt-msw9](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/49gt-msw9)
- **Tags**: `silicon quantum dot`  `nuclear spin qubit`  `germanium-73`  `hybrid quantum system`  `coherent control`

### TL;DR

Demonstrates coherent coupling of a single 73Ge nuclear spin to an electrostatically defined quantum dot in silicon, enabling a new hybrid qubit platform.

### Motivation

Nuclear spins in silicon offer long coherence times but are typically hosted by donor atoms; integrating them with quantum dots could combine the advantages of both platforms for scalable quantum computing.

### Method

The authors fabricate a silicon quantum dot device with a natural 73Ge isotope and use pulsed electron spin resonance and nuclear magnetic resonance techniques to coherently control the nuclear spin via the hyperfine interaction.

### Result

They achieve coherent manipulation of the 73Ge nuclear spin, measure its coherence time, and demonstrate the ability to initialize and read out the nuclear spin state through the quantum dot.

### Conclusion

This work establishes a new hybrid system coupling a nuclear spin to a quantum dot, opening avenues for long-lived quantum memories and multi-qubit registers in silicon.

*Keywords: silicon quantum dot, nuclear spin qubit, germanium-73, hybrid quantum system, coherent control*

## 7. Coarse-grained Quantum Thermodynamics: Observation-dependent Quantities, Observation-independent Laws

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: APS  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Research
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-11
- **Authors**: Giulia Rubino, Časlav Brukner, And Gonzalo Manzano
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/gcyn-18tv](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/gcyn-18tv)
- **Tags**: `thermodynamics`  `coarse-graining`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `observation-dependent`  `fluctuation theorems`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a coarse-grained framework for quantum thermodynamics where thermodynamic quantities are defined relative to the observer's measurement capabilities, while the laws of thermodynamics remain objective.

### Motivation

Standard thermodynamics assumes objective values for quantities like work and entropy, but in quantum systems, these depend on measurement context. The authors aim to reconcile observation-dependent quantities with universal thermodynamic laws.

### Method

They develop a coarse-graining approach based on the observer's measurement resolution, defining effective thermodynamic variables that depend on the coarse-graining scale, and derive fluctuation theorems and second laws that hold for any coarse-graining.

### Result

The framework yields observation-dependent work and entropy, yet the second law and fluctuation theorems remain valid for any coarse-graining, showing that thermodynamic laws are robust while quantities are context-dependent.

### Conclusion

Coarse-grained thermodynamics provides a consistent way to handle measurement dependence in quantum thermodynamics, with implications for quantum heat engines and information processing.

*Keywords: coarse-graining, quantum thermodynamics, observation-dependent, fluctuation theorems, second law*

### TL;DR

A general method for incorporating device characterization into composable security proofs for QKD, providing a rigorous framework for standards and certification.

### Motivation

### Method

The authors develop a general framework that integrates device characterization data (e.g., from calibration or tomography) into composable security proofs using entropy accumulation and smooth min-entropy techniques.

### Result

### Conclusion

This work bridges theory and practice, enabling more efficient and practical QKD implementations with certified security.

### TL;DR

A sequential weak measurement protocol for quantum frequency estimation that uses weakly entangled ancilla qubits to suppress phase slip errors and extend dynamic range, achieving Heisenberg scaling and saturating the Cramér-Rao bound.

### Motivation

Quantum frequency estimation is limited by phase slip errors that reduce dynamic range; existing methods trade off precision for range. The authors aim to overcome this using sequential weak measurements.

### Method

The protocol employs coherent spin states as sensing qubits and weakly entangled ancilla qubits that track the sensing qubits during free evolution. Bayesian estimation is performed from the weak measurement outcomes.

### Result

The protocol attains Heisenberg scaling with time and asymptotically saturates the Cramér-Rao precision bound across a large frequency bandwidth, effectively extending the dynamic range without sacrificing precision.

### Conclusion

Sequential weak measurements provide a practical way to enhance dynamic range in quantum frequency estimation while maintaining optimal precision, with potential applications in quantum sensing.

*Keywords: quantum frequency estimation, weak measurement, Heisenberg scaling, Cramér-Rao bound, entanglement*

### Motivation

Variational quantum algorithms suffer from barren plateaus, where gradients vanish exponentially with system size, hindering trainability. Understanding how noise affects loss function concentration is crucial for optimizing training.

### Method

The authors analyze noisy parametrized quantum circuits using tools from random matrix theory and quantum information theory to derive bounds on the variance of the loss function and its dependence on circuit depth, noise rate, and expressivity.

### Result

They show that noise can actually help by reducing the effective expressivity of the circuit, leading to less concentration of the loss function and improved trainability, quantified by a noise-induced effective depth.

### Conclusion

The results provide a roadmap for navigating barren plateaus by balancing noise and expressivity, offering practical guidelines for designing trainable variational quantum algorithms.

### Motivation

To understand the role of nonstabilizerness, a key resource for quantum computation, in molecular systems and to see if it reveals new features of chemical bonding.

### Method

The authors compute magic measures (e.g., stabilizer Rényi entropy) for electronic ground states of diatomic molecules along bond-stretching coordinates using ab initio quantum chemistry methods.

Magic measures form an intermediate-separation peak near rapid changes of the binding-energy curve, indicating that nonstabilizerness captures bonding features not captured by entanglement alone.

To understand how the density of active units affects the collective mechanical response in active solids.

Combined experiments with robotic metamaterials and theoretical modeling.

The phenomenon 'more is less' arises from percolation thresholds in active solids.

This paper uses Monte Carlo simulations and finite-size scaling to study phase behavior of intrinsically disordered proteins, focusing on criticality and binodal regimes.

### Motivation

Understanding phase transitions of prion-like low complexity domains is important for biomolecular condensate biogenesis, but the paper is purely biophysical without quantum or information-theoretic aspects.

### Method

Large-scale Monte Carlo simulations on lattices with Binder cumulants and finite-size scaling to map critical regime and compute binodals.

Accurate estimation of theta temperature requires direct calculation of the two-body interaction coefficient, not scaling from homopolymer theories.

### TL;DR

### Motivation

AlF molecules are promising for laser cooling and trapping, but efficient and continuous sources are needed for advanced experiments.

### Method

The authors built a compact molecular beam oven using a thermochemical reaction between AlF3 and Al, and also tested a dispenser package; they characterized beam brightness, rotational temperature, and velocity using spectroscopy.

### Result

The oven achieves high brightness (10^11 molecules/sr/s) at 923 K, outperforming ablation-based sources for certain levels; buffer gas cooling reduces rotational temperature to ~30 K and velocity to 200 m/s.

### Motivation

Neuroblastoma is a pediatric cancer with limited treatment options; identifying synthetic lethal targets like PLK4 in TRIM37-amplified tumors could lead to new therapies.

### Method

The authors tested RP-1664 in neuroblastoma cell lines and patient-derived xenografts, analyzing cell viability, centrosome dynamics, and tumor growth inhibition.

### Result

RP-1664 showed potent efficacy at both high doses (centrosome depletion, TRIM37-dependent) and low doses (centrosome amplification, TRIM37-independent), with strong tumor regression in preclinical models.

### Conclusion

RP-1664 is a promising therapeutic candidate for neuroblastoma, especially in tumors with TRIM37 gain, and its dual mechanism may overcome resistance.

### Method

The authors perform a combined spatial and energy-based analysis of gamma-ray data from the Galactic Center, using statistical techniques to infer the energy distribution of the sources.

### Result

### TL;DR

This paper reports a precise measurement of the chromoelectric dipole moment of the charm quark using CP-violating asymmetry in hadronic transitions.

### Motivation

Testing CP symmetry in the charm sector is important for understanding matter-antimatter asymmetry and probing new physics beyond the Standard Model.

### Method

The BESIII collaboration analyzed a large dataset of ψ(3770) decays to measure the CP-violating asymmetry in the transition ψ(3770) → D0 D̅0 → π+π−π0.

### Result

The measured asymmetry is consistent with zero, setting an upper limit on the charm quark chromoelectric dipole moment.

### Conclusion

No CP violation is observed, and the result provides the most stringent constraint on the charm quark chromoelectric dipole moment to date.

*Keywords: CP violation, chromoelectric dipole moment, charm quark, BESIII, hadronic transition*

### Method

### Result

The Newman-Janis algorithm is shown to have a definite physical origin in terms of instanton superposition, and the derivation extends to Kerr-Newman metrics.

### TL;DR

This paper reports a new ground state in neutron-rich lanthanum-149 that resolves an anomaly in two-neutron separation energies, providing insights into nuclear shell evolution.

### Motivation

Recent mass measurements of neutron-rich lanthanum isotopes revealed an unexpected prominence in two-neutron separation energies, which could not be explained by existing nuclear models. This anomaly suggested a possible change in ground-state configuration.

### Method

The authors performed precision mass measurements using a multi-reflection time-of-flight mass spectrograph (MRTOF-MS) at the RIKEN facility, combined with laser spectroscopy to determine the ground-state spin and parity.

### Result

They identified a new ground state in 149La with a different spin and parity than previously assumed, which removes the anomaly in the two-neutron separation energy trend and aligns with theoretical predictions.

### Conclusion

The discovery of the correct ground state in 149La resolves the observed anomaly and confirms the robustness of nuclear shell model predictions for neutron-rich lanthanum isotopes.

*Keywords: nuclear mass measurement, ground state, two-neutron separation energy, lanthanum isotopes, shell evolution*

### TL;DR

EXO-200 reports a search for double beta decays of 134Xe, finding no evidence and setting a lower limit on the half-life.

### Motivation

Double beta decay is a rare nuclear process that can probe neutrino properties, such as Majorana nature and mass hierarchy.

### Method

The EXO-200 detector, a liquid xenon time projection chamber, collected data over several years and used a likelihood analysis to search for the 0νββ and 2νββ decay modes of 134Xe.

### Result

No significant signal was observed; a lower limit on the half-life for 0νββ decay of 134Xe was set at 2.8×10^23 years (90% C.L.).

### Conclusion

The result improves previous limits and constrains nuclear models, but does not directly impact quantum information, thermodynamics, networks, or hybrid quantum systems.

*Keywords: double beta decay, 134Xe, EXO-200, neutrino, half-life*

### Motivation

### Method

The authors combine field measurements of fiber length distributions at the sea surface and at 5 m depth with a theoretical model of fiber rise velocity and turbulent mixing to explain the observed surface enrichment.

### Result

They find that the ratio of long to short fibers is significantly higher at the sea surface than at 5 m depth, and this can be explained by the balance between buoyant rise and turbulent diffusion.

### Conclusion

## 456. <i>Colloquium</i>: The Cosmic Dipole Anomaly

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: APS  |  **Feed**: Reviews of Modern Physics
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2025-12-11
- **Authors**: Nathan Secrest, Sebastian von Hausegger, Mohamed Rameez, Roya Mohayaee, And Subir Sarkar
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/9ygx-z2yq](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/9ygx-z2yq)
- **Tags**: `anisotropy`  `review`  `cosmic dipole`  `cosmological principle`  `ΛCDM`

### TL;DR

This paper reviews the cosmic dipole anomaly, a tension between the dipole in the cosmic microwave background and that in large-scale structure, challenging the cosmological principle.

### Motivation

To test the foundational cosmological principle of isotropy and homogeneity by comparing the dipole in galaxy/quasar catalogs with the CMB dipole.

### Method

Analysis of large catalogs of radio galaxies and quasars to measure the dipole in matter distribution and compare it with the CMB dipole, including assessment of biases and systematics.

### Result

A significant inconsistency is found between the matter dipole and the CMB dipole, which cannot be explained by the standard ΛCDM model.

### Conclusion

The cosmic dipole anomaly may indicate a violation of the cosmological principle or require new physics beyond ΛCDM.

*Keywords: cosmic dipole, cosmological principle, ΛCDM, large-scale structure, CMB*

## 464. Atomic-scale 3d Insights Into Heteroepitaxial Mechanisms In Hcp/fcc, Bcc/fcc, And Fcc/fcc Core–shell Nanoparticles

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-08
- **Authors**: Xilong Mu, Yao Zhang, Kang’an Wang, Haoyi Tang, Zhiheng Xie, Zezhou Li, Xuemin Cao, Peng He And Jihan Zhou
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae72c5](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae72c5)
- **Tags**: `strain`  `relaxation`  `defects`  `dislocation`  `heteroepitaxy`

### TL;DR

This paper uses atomic-resolution electron tomography to study heteroepitaxial mechanisms in core-shell nanoparticles with different crystal structures.

### Motivation

Understanding how interfacial defects and strain relaxation accommodate structural mismatch in heteroepitaxial nanoparticles is crucial for engineering atomic arrangements.

### Method

Atomic-resolution electron tomography was used to determine 3D atomic structures of Pt-based core-shell nanoparticles (hcp/fcc Ru@Pt, bcc/fcc PdCu@Pt, fcc/fcc Ru@Pt), combined with molecular statics simulations.

### Result

Distinct heteroepitaxial modes were identified: mixed coherent/semi-coherent growth, coherent growth with strain sharing, and oriented growth with twin proliferation; a 3D dislocation network below 10 nm was observed.

### Conclusion

The insights into heteroepitaxial mechanisms can inform targeted design of interfacial structures and strain engineering in core-shell nanomaterials.

*Keywords: heteroepitaxy, core-shell nanoparticles, electron tomography, dislocation network, strain engineering*

### TL;DR

This paper studies polyphosphate synthesis in bacteria during nutrient upshift, focusing on phosphate and ATP homeostasis.

### Motivation

Microorganisms face rapid nutrient fluctuations, and recovery from starvation is poorly understood.

### Method

Experimental study using bacterial cultures, measuring polyphosphate levels, ATP, and phosphate concentrations during nutrient upshift.

### Result

Polyphosphate synthesis is essential for maintaining phosphate and ATP homeostasis during recovery from starvation.

### Conclusion

Polyphosphate plays a key role in cellular adaptation to nutrient availability, but the work is purely biological and unrelated to quantum information or thermodynamics.

*Keywords: polyphosphate, ATP homeostasis, nutrient upshift, bacterial physiology*
